A causal budget allocator concentrates prefetches on high-probability outcomes, while uncovered outcomes fall back to sequential lookup without changing committed tokens. Across two model families and three reasoning and structured-generation workloads, NgramRL commits 3.60-7.77 tokens per target-model forward in the warm-cache regime, up to 1.99x more than SuffixDecoding and 7.0x more than prompt-lookup decoding. On Spider, the prefetcher covers 96.6% of Qwen3-8B outcomes at a candidate budget of 16 and improves measured rollout throughput by 1.38x over synchronous NgramRL, approaching its 1.40x overlap ceiling.


### [194] [Closing the Modality Gap via Simplex-Constrained Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32890)
*Shubham Gupta, Siva Reddy, Perouz Taslakian, Valentina Zantedeschi, Cem Subakan*

Main category: cs.AI

Abstract: While multimodal contrastive models like CLIP and CLAP successfully learn shared representations, their embeddings typically exhibit a persistent \emph{modality gap}. We propose addressing this separation through a purely geometric approach: changing the embedding domain from the unit sphere, $S^{d-1}$, to the probability simplex, $\Delta^{d-1}$. Although both manifolds share the same intrinsic dimension, the simplex acts as a structural prior; its nonnegative, unit-mass constraints serve as a cross-modal calibration that forces both modalities to ground themselves in the same coordinate system. By attaching lightweight softmax adapters and using Total Variation similarity, we reduce the centroid $\ell_2$ gap by 97--99\% across five multimodal retrieval benchmarks. This confirms that simplicial geometry induces genuine semantic alignment, matching or exceeding the retrieval performance of standard unconstrained models.


### [195] [SemantEq: Faithful Bidirectional Equivalence of Formal Statements](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32919)
*Xinrui Li, Pawan Sasanka Ammanamanchi, Siddharth Bhat, Rianna Jamdar, Yongxi Lin, Stella Biderman*

Main category: cs.AI

Abstract: Statement autoformalization translates mathematics from natural language into a proof assistant such as Lean 4, and models now do it at scale. As provers get stronger, the burden of correctness shifts from the proof to the statement, that is, to whether the formal statement faithfully translates its source. Given a trusted reference formalization, the natural test is logical equivalence, and it proves too much. $P \leftrightarrow Q$ holds trivially for any two statements $P$ and $Q$ that the library proves, because a prover can prove each separately and pass through $P \leftrightarrow \mathrm{True} \leftrightarrow Q$. The stronger the prover, the more unrelated pairs pass. Earlier methods restrict the prover and check that each proof names the other statement. We introduce SemantEq, a more principled relation that instead requires each statement to turn into the other only through a chain of allowed steps, such as logical rearrangement and rewriting with the library's own equations. Our programs judge a proof by the proof term Lean checks, not by its tactic script. SemantEq-Eval, a search without a model, reaches 100% precision and 65.6% recall on the ProofNetVerif test split under our adjudicated labels, against 95.8% and 61.8% for BEq+, and an 8.9-fold increase in its CPU time adds at most three pairs. A language model finds more proofs, but under two of four prompts it proves both directions of about two in five of the non-equivalent pairs. SemantEq-ProofAudit audits each model-written proof under a policy derived from the relation and accepts none of the 104 non-equivalent pairs, and it raises recall on 407 equivalent pairs from 65.4% to 81.1% under the best prompt. Separating the generation of equivalence proofs from their acceptance lets a stronger prover raise recall without changing what an acceptance means. We release code and data at: this https URL


<div id='cs.CE'></div>

# cs.CE [[Back]](#toc)

### [196] [Real-to-Sim Calibration of Bosch Etching Simulators with Few-Shot XSEM Data and Hybrid Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31904)
*Xinyu Jia, Jainil Shah, Rahul Tripathi, Vijay Gupta, Zhihong Chen*

Main category: cs.CE

Abstract: The Bosch process is widely used in MEMS and TSV fabrication, but accurate prediction of etching outcomes remains difficult without calibration against real measurements, which are sparse and costly to obtain. This work presents a few-shot real-to-sim calibration framework that decouples simulator parameters from direct process variables to infer a recipe-specific simulator parameter vector from sparse measurements, enabling reliable prediction of etch depth and sidewall angle under held-out process conditions. We first formulate Bosch profile prediction as a few-shot real-to-sim calibration problem, where a small set of real XSEM measurements is used to identify a recipe-specific ViennaPS parameter vector $z_r^*$ that minimizes the discrepancy between simulated and measured etch depth and sidewall angle. To search this parameter space efficiently, the framework combines local exploration, LLM-guided candidate generation, and XGBoost-based surrogate screening, while retaining ViennaPS simulation as the final verification step for each selected candidate. Experiments on two Bosch etching recipes show that the proposed framework accurately predicts held-out etching results, achieving normalized global errors of 0.340 and 0.249 for the 2 recipes respectively.


<div id='cond-mat.dis-nn'></div>

# cond-mat.dis-nn [[Back]](#toc)

### [197] [Generalization properties of the token perceptron: the role of embedding and vocabulary](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32788)
*Dario Bocchi, Matteo Negri, Flavio Nicoletti, Francesco D'Amico*

Main category: cond-mat.dis-nn

Abstract: Large language models are trained to predict tokens from a finite vocabulary, each represented by a vector in a $d$-dimensional embedding space. Therefore, token prediction is a classification problem whose classes have a geometry determined by the embedding. We study how this geometry affects learning in a minimal solvable model: a linear predictor that maps a sequence of $N$ embedded tokens to a $d$-dimensional output through one $d\times d$ matrix per position, trained in the teacher--student setting. We call this the \textit{token perceptron}. We compute its generalization error with the replica method, taking $N\to\infty$ at fixed number of examples per position $\alpha=P/N$ and keeping $d$ and the vocabulary size $D$ finite. As a first reference case, we consider continuous embeddings on the sphere, which isolate the role of the embedding dimension. The natural load is then $\alpha/d$, and for $d>1$ a normalized label fixes the direction of the teacher output, so the problem becomes a noiseless regression: the teacher is recovered exactly above $\alpha_{\rm rec}=d^2/(d-1)$, and spherical cross-entropy reaches this threshold at large inverse temperature. In the same setting the storage capacity of random associations scales as $d^2$ for margins $O(1/\sqrt{d})$. We then turn to vocabulary embeddings, where a label only identifies the Voronoi cell of the teacher output, so that each example imposes inequalities rather than equations, as in the classical perceptron. We compute the Bayes-optimal error for a given embedding matrix through an approximate spectral closure and show that the optimal learner first resolves the directions in which the vocabulary has the largest variance, even when the teacher is isotropic.


<div id='cs.NE'></div>

# cs.NE [[Back]](#toc)

### [198] [ENCL: Enabling Efficient Neuromorphic Continual Learning with Local Learning Rule and Sub-Network Freezing Strategy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31927)
*Rachmad Vidya Wicaksana Putra, Shuochen Zhou, Hassan Raza, Muhammad Shafique*

Main category: cs.NE

Abstract: Continual learning (CL) in neural networks is a promising learning approach for updating the knowledge of deployed systems based on new data encountered at run time, that have not been learned during the offline training phase. To enable their power/energy-efficient CL processing, neuromorphic continual learning (NCL) concept with sparse event-based operations have recently been introduced. However, state-of-the-art NCL methods typically employ the memory-hungry data/experience replay, coupled with costly global learning rule (i.e., backpropagation-based surrogate gradient). To address these limitations, we propose ENCL, a novel methodology that enables efficient NCL by employing a lightweight local learning rule and sub-network freezing strategy. ENCL leverages the temporally- and spatially-local learning rule to perform efficient credit assignment for training new tasks, alleviating the expensive cost of global learning rule. ENCL also employs a sub-network freezing strategy based on the given tasks to avoid the needs for network architecture expansion, hence further improving the efficiency and scalability of the NCL training process. To efficiently support this, we train each task with a small number of epochs as well as adjusted learning rate and timestep. Experiment results on task-incremental learning (TIL) scenario show that, our ENCL consistently achieves strong performance across different datasets as compared to state-of-the-art works. Specifically, ENCL achieves competitive accuracy comparable to that of the state-of-the-art data/experience replay, while reducing memory cost by up to 88%, NCL training time by up to 51%, average power consumption by up to 40%, and energy consumption by up to 65% across different benchmarking datasets (i.e., Split MNIST, Split Fashion MNIST, Split CIFAR-10, and Split CIFAR-100).


### [199] [RobustNCL: Robustifying Neuromorphic Continual Learning under Transient Faults with Lightweight Detect-and-Cure Strategy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31954)
*Rachmad Vidya Wicaksana Putra, Shuochen Zhou, Muhammad Shafique*

Main category: cs.NE

Abstract: In neural networks, continual learning (CL) is a prominent approach for enabling deployed systems to adapt to dynamic operational environments by effectively learning from new unseen data at runtime. Recently, the neuromorphic continual learning (NCL) concept has emerged as an energy-efficient alternative to CL by leveraging sparse event-based operations. However, NCL performance can be undermined by transient faults in the hardware platforms, which may come from high-energy particle strikes at any time. Currently, NCL reliability under transient faults has not been studied, and hence the corresponding fault-tolerance techniques remain unexplored. To address this, we propose a novel RobustNCL framework to improve NCL reliability in the presence of transient faults in the DRAM-based off-chip memory of hardware platforms through a lightweight detect-and-cure strategy. Specifically, it first monitors the presence of faults during weight data access from off-chip memory, and then cures the accessed weights if anomalous signatures are detected. It provides three curing variants: replacement with zero, min/max value, and most-probable value from the original/clean weight distribution. To support this, the weight curing values are stored in the radiation-hardened memory. Experimental results show that, in the presence of high fault rates (e.g., 5x10^{-4} for memory replay), RobustNCL maintains high accuracy close to the accuracy of fault-free models (i.e., within -0.5% accuracy for the best RobustNCL variant) and significantly improves accuracy from the baseline without fault mitigation across different NCL methods and datasets (i.e., Split MNIST, Split Fashion-MNIST, Split CIFAR-10, and Split CIFAR-100), while incurring negligible latency and energy overheads. These results highlight that our RobustNCL framework is a promising approach for enabling reliable and energy-efficient NCL systems.


<div id='cs.PL'></div>

# cs.PL [[Back]](#toc)

### [200] [On the Expressiveness of Asynchronous Processes with Nested Session Types](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32558)
*Sjors Wortelboer, Dan Frumin, Jorge A. Pérez*

Main category: cs.PL

Abstract: Session types are a type-based approach to correct message-passing programs, where types specify protocols for channels and type checking guarantees communication correctness. Traditional session types express regular protocols only, which leaves out many protocols found in practice. Aiming at overcoming this limitation, Nested Session Types (NSTs) can specify nested recursive protocols. NSTs are known to be characterised by one-counter machines and deterministic context-free languages. In this paper, we study the expressiveness of ASP (Asynchronous Session Processes), the process model associated with NSTs as introduced by Das et al. We prove that AST is Turing complete by giving a correct encoding of two-counter machines into typed processes. Our encoding uses a non-trivial state-aware representation of counters and instructions that is neatly governed by NSTs.


<div id='physics.soc-ph'></div>

# physics.soc-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [201] [Hybrid SEIR Temporal Fusion Transformer Framework with SHAP Analysis for Interpretable Pandemic Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31673)
*Ashish Anton Abraham, Gopi Krishnan R, Aiswarya Anil Nair, Ajo Babu George, Kunal Agarwal*

Main category: physics.soc-ph

Abstract: Forecasting pandemic spread across heterogeneous regions demands models that are both accurate and interpretable for public health action. This paper proposes a hybrid forecasting framework that integrates an SEIR epidemiological model enhanced with a Temporal Fusion Transformer (TFT) to produce interpretable multi-horizon infection forecasts. Using region-wise COVID-19 data from the Our World in Data corpus, we present a unified pipeline that integrates epidemiological indicators (susceptible, exposed, infectious, recovered), intervention signals (masking, vaccination, policies), mobility covariates, and demographic context. The SEIR component estimates latent disease dynamics and injects mechanistic priors, while the TFT learns non-linear temporal dependencies and cross-feature interactions from historical observations and known future inputs. To ensure transparency, SHAP (SHapley Additive exPlanations) is applied at both global and local levels, quantifying feature contributions and revealing region-specific drivers of surges and slowdowns. Compared with a calibrated standalone SEIR baseline, the hybrid SEIR-TFT approach provides more stable and accurate forecasts across epidemic phases, particularly under shifting intervention regimes. The framework is further operationalized through structured prediction and explanation outputs for interactive dashboards, enabling time-series, policy-impact, and geospatial analysis. By unifying mechanistic modeling, deep temporal learning, and explainable AI, this study offers a practical decision-support system for policy-sensitive pandemic forecasting.


<div id='eess.AS'></div>

# eess.AS [[Back]](#toc)

### [202] [MG-REPA: Multi-Granularity Representation Alignment for Generative Target Speaker Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32161)
*Dohwan Kim, Jung-Woo Choi*

Main category: eess.AS

Abstract: Generative target speaker extraction (TSE) models achieve high-quality extraction, but suffer from considerably slow training convergence. Representation Alignment (REPA) accelerates diffusion transformer training by aligning hidden states with a pre-trained Self-Supervised Learning (SSL) teacher. However, existing alignment in the speech and audio domain operates at a single granularity. For TSE, frame-level alignment leaves the global, time-invariant characteristics of the target speech implicit, whereas utterance-level alignment leaves the time-varying content implicit, even though extracting the target requires both. We propose Multi-Granularity Representation Alignment (MG-REPA), aligning the hidden states with an SSL model at the frame level and a speaker verification model at the utterance level. We further apply temporal normalization to the frame-level target, removing its utterance-level statistics so that each branch concentrates on its granularity. On Libri2Mix, MG-REPA reaches the OVRL and PESQ of a MeanFlow-TSE baseline with $3.0\times$ and $2.1\times$ fewer epochs, respectively. Trained for 750 epochs, it is competitive with the published 2,000-epoch baseline on intrusive metrics while improving non-intrusive metrics. An ablation demonstrates that MG-REPA outperforms alignment at a single granularity.


### [203] [Uni-Stream: A Unified Streaming Speech Language Model for Speech Synthesis and Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32187)
*Xuan Shi, Chang Zeng, Tiantian Feng, Ye Zhang, Jun Liu, Shih-Heng Wang, Lie Lu, Shrikanth Narayanan*

Main category: eess.AS

Abstract: Unified streaming speech language models aim to support speech recognition and synthesis within a single model, enabling real-time human-computer interaction. However, jointly supporting these two directions of speech-text conversion while preserving task-specific performance remains challenging. We propose Uni-Stream, a unified streaming speech language model built on compact pretrained speech models. Uni-Stream processes interleaved text and speech in a streaming format and achieves performance comparable to task-specific models through two-stage training. We also develop a data annotation pipeline for streaming training and will publicly release the generated transcriptions and word-level timestamps. We further examine Uni-Stream's representations across tasks and modalities and find that speech and text residence in distinct latent feature space even when trained in a unified model architecture, while representations within each modality become more task-dependent in later layers.


<div id='econ.TH'></div>

# econ.TH [[Back]](#toc)

### [204] [Monetizing Intelligence: Advertising and Subscription Design on LLM Platforms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32945)
*Yuhang Ji, Fasheng Xu, Tianjun Feng*

Main category: econ.TH

Abstract: Large language model (LLM) platforms introducing advertising must decide how much capability ad revenue should fund and what consumers should pay to avoid ads. We study a monopolistic platform that jointly chooses costly response quality, advertising intensity, and subscription prices when advertising reduces user value but better service raises advertising returns. Three findings follow. First, when partly ad-supported and ad-free plans coexist, advertising can finance useful service for consumers who receive none under pure subscription. Lower effective quality in middle plans weakens the alternatives available to premium users, allowing higher prices for unchanged ad-free service. Some fully ad-supported users gain utility, while partly ad-supported and ad-free users lose: broader access can coexist with utility polarization. Second, an intermediate advertising cap can maximize consumer surplus by preserving some revenue that finances service while limiting degradation. Third, cheaper compute can raise quality and reduce ad intensity, yet sufficiently large cost reductions can increase aggregate advertising revenue above its initial level because better service raises the value of the remaining advertised interactions. An extension shows how advertising-funded upgrades can bring plans to the same maximum capability and advertising exposure, eliminating the basis for different prices. These results make menu design central to evaluating LLM advertising: the consequences extend beyond users who see ads to the alternatives and prices faced by those who do not.


<div id='cs.IT'></div>

# cs.IT [[Back]](#toc)

### [205] [StarRTT: An End-to-End Real-Time Framework for Blind Zone-Free RTT Prediction in Starlink LEO Satellite Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32540)
*Jaeyong Lee, Seongjin Jeong, Yusang Park, Hyunho Hwang, Sangmin Han, Wonjae Shin*

Main category: cs.IT

Abstract: Low Earth Orbit (LEO) satellite mega-constellations such as Starlink require frequent handovers due to high orbital velocities, resulting in substantial Round-Trip Time (RTT) fluctuations. Accurate RTT prediction can mitigate these fluctuations, yet it fundamentally relies on continuously updated serving- satellite information. Existing serving-satellite identification methods typically complete identification only at the end of each scheduling cycle, creating an identification blind zone that limits real-time prediction. To overcome this limitation, we develop StarRTT, an end-to-end real-time framework that eliminates the blind zone by integrating per-second serving-satellite identification with RTT prediction. Within this framework, we propose Real-Time Long Short-Term Memory (RT-LSTM), an LSTM-based RTT prediction model designed to capture both non-stationary RTT evolution and time-varying geometric relationships among the serving satellite, user terminal, and gateways. Specifically, a historical LSTM encodes historical RTT observations and real-time serving-satellite and gateway-relative features, while a future LSTM processes Two-Line Element (TLE)-propagated future features of the identified serving satellite up to the next scheduling instant. Under strict real-time back-testing, RT-LSTM achieves a Mean Absolute Error (MAE) of 1.405 ms averaged over all 15 prediction horizons and 15 reference time-slot indices, reducing the error by 4.3 %, 11.6 %, and 22.1 % compared with the existing baseline models, LSTM, StarNet, and Attention-based Spatiotemporal Model (ASTM), respectively. Notably, the reduction over LSTM reaches 12.5 % at the scheduling instant, highlighting the benefit of real-time serving-satellite information when recent RTT observations are limited.


<div id='eess.SP'></div>

# eess.SP [[Back]](#toc)

### [206] [EMG Tokenization Preserves Movement Information at a Fraction of the Bitrate](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32674)
*Raul C. Sîmpetru, Amin Olamazadeh, Devon Rohlf, Vlad Cnejevici, Alessandro Del Vecchio*

Main category: eess.SP

Abstract: Wireless electromyography (EMG) interfaces must balance electrode coverage against transmission bandwidth. Here, we show that compact EMG tokens substantially reduce payload while retaining information for three-dimensional hand-pose decoding. We evaluate two causal tokenizers fitted without pose labels: an encoder pretrained to reconstruct EMG spectra and one based on SPAR, an engineered spectral-spatial representation of neighbouring electrodes. In personalized, within-session experiments with 320-channel EMG from nine participants, we compare tokenized and continuous features using matched decoder architectures and training procedures. Averaged across decoders, tokenization changed mean error by +0.01 mm for SPAR (95% confidence interval: -0.12 to 0.14) and +0.45 mm for the learned representation (0.10 to 0.82). Both increases met a 1 mm non-inferiority criterion. Stored token arrays reduced payloads 41-fold relative to raw EMG and 36-fold relative to continuous SPAR features. On sampled windows of the public emg2pose benchmark's test partition, absolute differences in mean error between learned tokens and continuous features were below 0.05 mm across 176 participants and in the subset of 20 participants unseen during training. Electrode coverage, calibration, and unseen recordings affected SPAR decoding more than quantization did. In a computational benchmark, optimized SPAR generated tokens for 63 channels within 20 ms on a Cortex-M55 microcontroller. These findings support tokenization as a compact interface between wearable sensing and movement decoding, allowing decoders to be updated while the wearable encoder and transmitted representation remain fixed.


<div id='eess.SY'></div>

# eess.SY [[Back]](#toc)

### [207] [Heterogeneous Space Data Centers: A Key Paradigm for Future Non-Terrestrial and In-Orbit Services](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32260)
*Peng Hu, Mustafa Alhassan, Amjad Iqbal*

Main category: eess.SY

Abstract: Lower space-launch cost and advances in AI are driving space-based data centers (SDCs) that provide AI-centric computing and communication services to in-orbit and non-terrestrial assets. However, architectural principles, service models, and energy provisioning for such SDCs, particularly in low-Earth orbit (LEO), remain largely unexplored. We introduce heterogeneous SDCs composed of large and small nodes, differentiated by computing capability, size, and power budget, to support applications spanning ground, aerial, and in-orbit users. We define use cases, node sizing and placement strategies, and interconnection topologies integrating space-to-space and space-to-ground links. Focusing on dawn-dusk LEO configurations, we analyze solar power availability, visibility windows, and latency-aware coverage. Using baseline workloads for typical scenarios, we evaluate latency and service reliability. We quantify energy requirements of different SDC roles and deployment scales, highlighting trade-offs among node size, orbit selection, and service portfolio, and provide design guidance and future research directions for AI infrastructure in space.


### [208] [State Estimation as In-context Tabular Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32487)
*Ayushi Jolotia, Parikshit Pareek*

Main category: eess.SY

Abstract: This letter poses state estimation (SE) as an in-context tabular prediction (ICTP) problem. The key idea is a tabular arrangement in which historical measurement vectors and corresponding state estimates serve as the context, and a new measurement vector forms the query for which we predict a state vector. We propose to use tabular foundation models (TFMs), pretrained on synthetic data, whose weights are never updated, so power system data is needed only as context. With frozen TFM weights we estimate the state of 14-, 30-, 33-, 70-, and 118-bus transmission and distribution networks, and do so where weighted least squares (WLS) fails or degrades, under low observability and with bad data. ICTP errors are 11 to 57$\times$ below the per-bus average of the context at full metering, and 1.3 to 4.0$\times$ below it with only 5% of measurements available. With measurements at six buses biased by 8$\sigma$, the ICTP angle error rises 1.8 to 5.0$\times$ over its bias-free value against 2.5 to 12.7$\times$ for WLS with bad-data detection. Without retraining, ICTP has lower voltage error than DSS$^2$ on both of its feeders, and lower than a retrained TAPINE on two of three reconfiguration sets.


### [209] [Cross-Scenario Controller Parameter Transformation from Real-World Driving Data through Flow Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32719)
*Christian Zink, Maik Pfefferkorn, Eric Lenz, Felix Kallmeyer, Rolf Findeisen*

Main category: eess.SY

Abstract: Advanced Driver Assistance Systems have become increasingly widespread, aiming to support drivers, reduce workload, and improve traffic safety. However, many systems are rarely used because their behavior feels unnatural and does not match individual driving behavior. The growing availability of large-scale, real-world human driving data enables more human-like and driver-individual assistance functions. This paper formulates the adaptation of Adaptive Cruise Control parameters as a driver-individual scenario-to-scenario transport problem, transforming controller parameters from one driving context to another. Using flow matching, we train a generative model to predict the target-scenario behavior of previously unseen drivers from only a few demonstrations in a source scenario. We compare the approach with a linear parameter-shift baseline and evaluate flow-matching variants incorporating driver-consistent coupling, driver embeddings, and classifier-free guidance based on driving profiles extracted from trajectory data using a variational autoencoder.


<div id='stat.ML'></div>

# stat.ML [[Back]](#toc)

### [210] [Implicit Neural Representations for Variational Problems on Graphons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32147)
*Taeyoung Kim, Jineon Baek, Joonkyung Lee, Hongseok Yang*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: We propose a neural exploratory framework for variational problems over graphons, i.e., symmetric measurable functions $[0,1]^2 \to [0,1]$, that arise as limits of dense graph sequences. Such problems are central to two parts of the mathematical literature: extremal graph theory, which studies graph parameter optimisation under given restrictions, often for those graphs with a large number of vertices, and the large deviation theory of dense random graphs, which characterises the structure of rare events through constrained graphon optimisation. We represent graphons by implicit neural networks and optimise graphon objectives by gradient descent. Our design combines three ingredients: a multi-scale sinusoidal residual architecture biased toward sharp, step-like graphons; an embedded solver that enforces a single empirical density constraint and is differentiated by implicit differentiation; and symmetry-aware Monte Carlo estimators. On generalised Turán problems that previously required substantial human effort, our framework rediscovers known optimal graphons without human intervention. Applied to open instances, it produces candidate optima, including a previously unreported family of extremal structures. Applied to the variational problems from the large deviation theory of Erdős--Rényi random graphs, the framework produces new candidate optimal graphons across both upper- and lower-tail regimes for multiple pattern graphs; for the case of the triangle graph and the upper-tail regime, these candidates improve upon the best-known reference construction of Lubetzky and Zhao. The structures found by our framework have also led to new theorems on the optimal graphons for both families of problems.


### [211] [Autoregressive Physics-Informed Neural Operator Surrogates and Joint 4-D Seismic-Production Bayesian History Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31677)
*Clement Etienam, David Blom, Hari Pattathil, Tsubasa Onishi, Harpreet Sethi, Lalson Vincent, Suraj Pawar, Kishan Ramesh Kumar, Pandu Devarakota, Faruk O. Alpak*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: We present a surrogate-accelerated Bayesian history-matching framework, demonstrated on a realistic synthetic model ($N=100{,}188$ cells). The forward map $\mathcal{G}$ is replaced by a Fourier neural operator $\mathcal{G}_\theta$ trained in a closed loop, $\hat{x}_{n+1}=\mathcal{G}_\theta(u,\hat{x}_n)$, with the discrete black-oil residual $R(x_{n+1},x_n)$ as a physics-informed neural operator (PINO) penalty, truncated backpropagation and noise injection. We assimilate production data and 4-D seismic using adaptive regularised ensemble Kalman inversion (aREKI), and synthesise $\Delta V_p$ inside the rollout via a closed-form Gassmann transform of the surrogate, under a two-stage schedule (aREKI on production, then an ensemble smoother with multiple data assimilation, ES-MDA, on both). To temper the ill-posedness, we invert $(\log K,\phi)$ not in $\mathbb{R}^{2N}$, where $N_e\ll 2N$ leaves a rank-deficient covariance and a null space the data never inform, but on a chart $u=\Psi(\tilde m)$, $\tilde m\in\mathbb{R}^{d}$, $d\ll 2N$, given by a Parseval-exact truncated cosine transform or a variational auto-encoder decoder. The ensemble then spans its own search space; the directions the data cannot constrain are cut to those the chart can express, and every member decodes to a geologically plausible field. For the rollout we prove an exposure bound $W_1\le\sum_j L^{\,n-1-j}\mathbb{E}e_j$, a physics-penalty contraction $\|D_x\mathcal{G}_\theta\|\le\alpha<1$ under a small residual defect, and an $\mathcal{O}(\rho^K)$ truncation bias. A Loewner-order inequality $\mathcal{I}_p\preceq\mathcal{I}_p+\mathcal{I}_s$ explains why joint assimilation is no less informative. On held-out data, $\mathcal{G}_\theta$ attains $R^2>0.999$ on pressure and saturation and rolls out in $0.579$ s, a $\sim2500\times$ speed-up, bringing an $N_e=190$-member, $N_a=10$-iteration joint inversion within one GPU-hour.


### [212] [Early learning in large residual networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31783)
*Vladimir Narovlansky, Boris Hanin*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: We study the early training dynamics of nonlinear residual networks with blocks of zero hidden layers as a model of more general residual networks. We analyze networks in a sequential limit where first width and depth diverge, then the number of training data points diverges, and finally the input dimension diverges. This is a more refined setup compared to the typical mean-field theory analysis in which depth and width diverge but the number of training datapoints and input dimension are fixed. Our main technical result is a diagrammatic computation of both the network output and the hidden representations after the few steps of gradient descent in terms of the statistics of inner products of inputs from the training set. One of our principle conceptual findings is a negative result: in the limit of large input dimension the network output can learn only a linear function of its inputs early in training since higher learned monomials are more and more suppressed in the input dimension and vanish in the large dimension limit. This suggests that mean-field limits of ResNets are surprisingly rigid in the regime of high input dimension and may not be sufficient toy models for understanding feature learning. Finally, we investigate how to ensure hyperparameter transfer for the learning rate as a function of dataset size and input dimension. We test our predictions numerically and find excellent agreement with our theory.


### [213] [Combinatorial Characterizations of the Learnability of Conditional Distributions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31890)
*Mihir Dhanakshirur, Ambuj Tewari*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: We study the PAC-learnability of function classes that map inputs to probability distributions over a finite space. The learner observes i.i.d. input-label pairs, with each label sampled from the conditional distribution of an unknown target hypothesis in the class. We measure learning through excess risk under a bounded proper loss that satisfies two mild assumptions. We introduce the \textbf{fat$_\gamma$Natarajan dimension}, which generalizes both fat$_\gamma$ and Natarajan dimension, and prove that its finiteness at every positive scale is necessary and sufficient for learnability. We also introduce a \textbf{fat$_\gamma$Graph} dimension extending the classical \gra dimension and establish inequalities comparing the two dimensions at different scales, leading to an equivalent characterization of learnability. Finally, we show that a distribution-valued function class is learnable if and only if each of its coordinate classes is learnable in the probabilistic concept model.


### [214] [Conditional Flow Models for Offline Maximum Entropy Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32125)
*Hao Cheng, Yuling Jiao, Lican Kang, Jerry Zhijian Yang, Chen Zhong*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Offline maximum entropy reinforcement learning seeks to estimate the optimal soft action-value function \(Q^*\) from a fixed dataset without further interaction with the environment. Although \(Q^*\) is characterized by the soft optimal Bellman equation, the corresponding Bellman operator cannot be evaluated directly because both the reward distribution and the transition kernel are unknown. Consequently, estimating \(Q^*\) requires the joint treatment of environment modeling and value function learning. We propose a framework that addresses these tasks separately. Conditional diffusion models based on probability flow ODEs estimate the conditional reward distribution and transition kernel. The resulting distributional estimators define a data driven approximation of the soft optimal Bellman operator, whose empirical Bellman residual is minimized over a class of deep neural networks to estimate \(Q^*\). We establish nonasymptotic guarantees for the proposed method. For operator learning, we derive total variation error bounds for the conditional flow estimators that account explicitly for score estimation, numerical discretization, and terminal smoothing. For soft value learning, we first use the soft Bellman equation to show that $Q^*$ inherits Hölder regularity from the conditional reward and transition densities. For any Hölder index $\beta$, the value learning stage achieves an excess Bellman residual risk bound of order $\widetilde{\mathcal O}\big(n^{-\frac{2\beta}{d+2\beta}}\big),$ where $d$ is the total dimension of the state and action spaces. In contrast to many existing theoretical analyses of deep reinforcement learning, our results do not require Bellman completeness. Numerical experiments support the theoretical findings and demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method.


### [215] [Federated Nonparametric Regression with Heterogeneous Clients](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32178)
*Xiaoran Jiang, Anand N. Vidyashankar*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Whether a federated predictor benefits an individual client depends on both population heterogeneity and estimation uncertainty. We study this question for Nadaraya--Watson regression in a triangular array with potentially different fitting and evaluation distributions. Each client transmits local kernel summaries, allowing the server to construct a covariate-density-weighted federated estimator without centralizing individual observations. A client combines its fitted local predictor with the federated predictor through a prescribed weight, and gain is the paired reduction in squared prediction loss on an independent audit observation. Exact identities characterize both the gain of the realized fitted pair and its repeated-fitting mean. We also establish pointwise inference for the federated estimator. A two-stage analysis of client gains separates fitting variability from audit variability, retains the covariance created when a client's observations enter both its local estimator and the common server estimator, and yields fixed-block simultaneous inference across clients. Robustness is examined under two perturbation models. A Kullback--Leibler neighborhood provides a variance-sensitive lower certificate in a regular positive-gain-variance regime, while an $L_2$ neighborhood yields an exact least-favorable regression direction and robust value. Exact-model, vanishing-variance, and exact-zero-direction boundaries are kept separate because their scalar inputs are nonregular. The framework thus distinguishes decisions about a realized fitted pair from claims about the learning procedure under repeated fitting.


### [216] [Empirical concentration for chains with infinite memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32265)
*Pierre Alquier, Paul Doukhan*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Concentration inequalities for time series usually involve unknown quantities measuring dependence. This prevents their direct use as confidence bounds, or as criteria for learning algorithms. Recent works proved empirical concentration inequalities for stationary Markov chains, where the unknown quantities are estimated. We extend this approach beyond the Markov case, by considering stationary chains with infinite memory. We first establish a bounded-differences inequality on bounded Polish metric spaces under a summable Wasserstein contraction condition. We then estimate its dependence coefficients from a single trajectory, using either transition counts when the state space is finite, or derivatives of kernel conditional-density estimates for a continuous state space. We give general error bounds and illustrate their dependence on the memory tail. Finally, a fixed multiplicative safety factor turns these estimators into empirical concentration bounds. While some regularity assumptions are required to make the bounds empirical, our procedure remains fully adaptive in the sense that it does not require the knowledge of the constants involved in these assumptions. The empirical confidence bounds derived from our results are asymptotically as tight as the theoretical ones.


### [217] [Exact Global Minimax Risk near Nonidentification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32546)
*Kaiyue Yang, Yunan Wu*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: We study squared-error estimation when a nuisance parameter is unidentified on a submodel but affects a target that vanishes there. For models with higher-order profile expansions, we give sufficient conditions under which the largest critical-path minimax risk is the exact asymptotic minimax risk over the full parameter space. An efficient observable feature, negligible calibration error and stable recovery yield one estimator attaining the leading constant uniformly, with the profile coordinates unknown. The estimator uses calibration estimates, a local feature mean and an empirical recovery feature. The constant is expressed through a bounded-normal decision problem. We verify the conditions in two models. In centered Gaussian logistic errors-in-variables regression with one surrogate and two unknown variances, we determine the fifth-order global coefficient risk and its exact constant. A sparse extension separates variance-decomposition uncertainty from direction estimation and specifies when the scalar constant is preserved. In a degree-balanced replicated-network model, we determine the sixth-order global edge-contrast risk when block proportions are unknown. Triangles identify a parameter combination; a bowtie supplies the additional information needed for the contrast.


### [218] [Batched NN 4D-Var: a performant hybrid model framework for the Integrated Forecasting System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32582)
*Alban Farchi, Marcin Chrust, Massimo Bonavita, Marc Bocquet, Wei Pan, Patrick Laloyaux*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Hybrid forecasting systems combining machine learning and numerical weather prediction offer a promising route toward improved forecast skill while preserving physical consistency. In this study, we investigate online learning of state-dependent model-error corrections within the Integrated Forecasting System using 4D-Var. Building on previous work, we represent model error using a neural network embedded within the forecast model and trained from observations through the data assimilation cycle. First, we introduce a targeted variant of neural-network 4D-Var in which only the neural-network parameters are estimated, while the atmospheric state is provided by a precomputed analysis. We then propose a batched extension that processes assimilation windows in random order and averages parameter increments over multiple windows, drawing inspiration from mini-batch stochastic optimization. Experiments are conducted with the Integrated Forecasting System of the European Centre for Medium-Range Weather Forecasts at an intermediate resolution. Sequential online learning substantially improves forecasts within the training period, with error reductions exceeding 10%in some cases. However, these improvements fail to generalize to unseen data. In contrast, the proposed batched formulation produces hybrid models that consistently improve forecast skill in out-of-sample experiments. Larger batch sizes accelerate convergence and improve robustness by reducing the noise of parameter updates. Diagnostics further show that the online-trained corrections preserve realistic forecast variability and do not improve skill through artificial smoothing. These results demonstrate that the formulation of the online learning problem is critical for generalisation and establish batched online training as a promising framework for hybrid machine-learning/data-assimilation systems.


### [219] [Acquisition for Spherical-Linear Bayesian Optimization on the Cube](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32628)
*Nathan Wycoff*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Recent work demonstrated that executing Bayesian Optimization (BO) using simply a linear surrogate with a clever transformation is enough to approximate the performance of the standard Gaussian-Process (GP) based version on high-dimensional test functions. When the decision region is a sphere, this strategy admits rapid exact acquisition. However, it is the unit cube we encounter most often in the BO literature, and in this setting a standard approach, namely a multi-start quasi-Newton method, is employed for solving the acquisition subproblem. In this article, we will discuss a series of results, both positive and negative, regarding the complexity of global resolution of various acquisition subproblems over the unit cube. On the basis of this discussion, we will propose a new acquisition function which is globally optimizable using significantly less execution time than the incumbent approach while maintaining or improving performance. The resulting method is competitive with GP-based BO in terms of accuracy per evaluation, and is superior in terms of accuracy per walltime on black-boxes of low to moderate computational intensity.


### [220] [Score-Preserving Continual Lasso under Task Shift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32708)
*Jingyi Guo, Runze Li, Lingzhou Xue*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Continual learning (CL) aims to learn from sequential tasks while mitigating catastrophic forgetting when historical data are no longer accessible. We study continual learning for high-dimensional sparse linear regression, where $\ell_1$ regularization creates a difficulty absent from continual ridge regression: that recursively reusing an $\ell_1$-penalized estimator can propagate earlier shrinkage and accumulate regularization bias across tasks. We propose score-preserving continual Lasso (SP-CL), which separates sparse estimation from state transmission by preserving the historical squared-loss score. Under squared loss, its cumulative estimator exactly recovers a full-history Lasso minimizer. When task coefficients coincide, it inherits the full-history sparse rate. For related task-specific coefficients, SP-CL uses a regularized current-task correction that leaves the transmitted state unchanged and attains the offline source-assisted rate under an oracle related-task condition. We also derive Overall/BWT/FWT decompositions that distinguish historical information loss, task conflict, and current-task specialization, and validate the resulting regimes in simulation.


### [221] [Sparse Bayesian Neural Networks via Sticky Piecewise Deterministic Markov Processes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32753)
*August Arnstad, Geir Olve Storvik, Filippo Pagani, Leiv Rønneberg*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: There is growing evidence that the performance of a neural network depends largely on a small subset of its weights, which are typically identified by training a dense network and pruning it afterwards. Within a Bayesian framework, pruning can be directly incorporated through a spike-and-slab prior, providing a proper treatment of the uncertainties involved, both with respect to pruning and to parameter uncertainty. In Bayesian neural networks (BNNs), sampling by MCMC is notoriously hard. We demonstrate that modern Monte Carlo methods based on piecewise deterministic Markov processes (PDMPs) can be used to sample posteriors of BNNs asymptotically exact whenever numerically constructed rate bounds hold. Spike-and-slab priors can be incorporated through sticky versions of the PDMPs, setting weights exactly to zero during sampling. We examine the predictive performance and convergence of the samplers, and show that sparse BNNs sampled this way can be more robust to input noise than dense ones.


### [222] [Singular Geometry of Low-Rank Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32854)
*Kamillo Ferry, Anthea Monod, Keisuke Yano*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Low-rank adaptation (LoRA) is now a standard approach to parameter-efficient fine-tuning of large neural networks. Its low-rank factorization is inherently non-identifiable, making LoRA a singular statistical model whose learning complexity cannot be described by parameter dimension alone. We study this complexity using the real log canonical threshold (RLCT) from singular learning theory and how it depends on both the low-rank parameterization and the pretrained network. Under a local linearization, LoRA induces a low-rank trace regression model whose Kullback--Leibler geometry is controlled by a forward-backward covariance, constructed from activations and backpropagated sensitivities at the adapted layer. This covariance determines which low-rank perturbations are statistically observable. When it is nondegenerate along the relevant low-rank directions, we recover the classical learning complexity of reduced-rank regression; when its kernel meets these directions, additional statistically indistinguishable parameters change the singular geometry. We characterize this geometry locally through observable tangent directions and a residual singularity, and globally through affine linear sections of determinantal varieties. The global learning complexity need not be determined at a ground-truth parameter, as singularities on another rank stratum can dominate. We also introduce a curvature-corrected multi-temperature estimator of the RLCT. Experiments on multilayer perceptron and transformer architectures reveal strongly layer-dependent forward-backward geometry and show that ambient covariance degeneracy does not imply degeneracy along the low-rank directions relevant to adaptation.


<div id='math.NA'></div>

# math.NA [[Back]](#toc)

### [223] [Norm-optimal model order reduction in reproducing kernel Hilbert spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28224)
*Tamás Dózsa, Andrea Angino, Rolf Krause, Matthias Voigt*

Main category: math.NA

Abstract: We consider the problem of model order reduction (MOR) in reproducing kernel Hilbert spaces (RKHSs). We prove generalizations of well-known necessary interpolatory conditions for norm-optimal reduced-order models, originally stated for rational functions in $H_2$ Hardy-Hilbert spaces. We construct an alternative to the iterative rational Krylov (IRKA) algorithm for RKHSs with differentiable kernels to obtain norm-optimal reduced-order models. Then, we introduce generalized Malmquist-Takenaka (GMT) functions that can be used to define orthonormal bases in an arbitrary RKHS. Using GMT functions, we propose unitary transformations between separable RKHSs. This allows us to extend our optimality conditions and subsequent algorithms to MOR problems posed in RKHSs with non-differentiable kernels. The potential and versatility of the proposed MOR framework is demonstrated in several numerical experiments. We perform norm-optimal MOR on various machine learning models such as support vector machines and deep ReLU networks. In addition, we show how the proposed MOR framework can be used to solve seemingly unrelated problems. In particular, we provide an example for fitting high degree polynomials to data and also obtain a FEM-like method for solving partial differential equations (PDEs) with an optimal kernel basis.


### [224] [Universal neural network approximation of symmetric polyconvex energies and envelopes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32598)
*Florian Gammer*

Main category: math.NA

Abstract: Symmetric polyconvexity provides a sufficient condition for weak lower semicontinuity in small-strain elasticity and a tractable lower bound for the relaxation of non-(quasi)convex energies. Since classical algorithms for computing such functions can be computationally expensive, neural network approximations have recently emerged as an alternative. A central difficulty with establishing such neural network based approaches for symmetric polyconvexity is the additional monotonicity property. As we show, the corresponding weight restrictions on ReLU-based input-convex networks do not yield universal approximation of the lifted representers. Here, we introduce symmetric polyconvex neural networks (SPCNNs) for constitutive modeling and approximation of symmetric polyconvex envelopes. The deep architecture combines convexity with the monotonicity required of a representer in the space of minors. We prove a universal approximation theorem for symmetric polyconvex energies, without requiring isotropy, and quantify the number of affine pieces attainable by the lifted network and derive reduced architectures for isotropic energies. Numerical examples illustrate stress-based regression and approximation of the symmetric polyconvex envelope of a double-well potential. Symmetric polyconvexity is guaranteed by construction, while the accuracy of the fitted energies and envelopes is assessed numerically.


<div id='cs.RO'></div>

# cs.RO [[Back]](#toc)

### [225] [PrefO2O: Zero-Shot Preference-Based Reinforcement Learning via Offline-to-Online Latent Preference Transfer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31812)
*Ruiqi Wang, Dezhong Zhao, Xuesu Xiao, Erdem Biyik, Byung-Cheol Min, Guohua Chen*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Preference-based reinforcement learning (PbRL) has emerged as a promising paradigm for learning robot behaviors aligned with human intent. However, its scalability is fundamentally limited by the need to collect expensive human preference labels for every new task. In this work, we present PrefO2O, an offline-to-online latent preference transfer framework that enables zero-shot PbRL on unseen target tasks without requiring any human feedback. The core idea is to learn a task-invariant latent preference space from a small amount of offline preference data on source tasks, where transferable preference structures are disentangled from task-specific variations. This learned manifold allows PrefO2O to transfer preference structures and reward priors to new tasks under distribution shift, while confidence-weighted optimal transport alignment generates reliable pseudo-preference supervision for online reward adaptation. Extensive experiments on robotic manipulation and locomotion benchmarks demonstrate that PrefO2O substantially outperforms existing preference-transfer baselines and matches or exceeds PbRL methods trained with real human feedback, while generalizing across multi-to-one and one-to-one transfer settings as well as across different robot embodiments.


### [226] [SEVI: Safe Evidential Real-World Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31829)
*Shaekh Mohammad Shithil, Lantao Liu*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Real-world reinforcement learning has enabled contact-rich manipulation tasks to be trained directly on physical robots, but unsafe interactions during online learning remain a key barrier to practical deployment. We propose Safe Evidential Real-World Reinforcement Learning (SEVI), a framework for reliability-aware safety intervention during real-world policy learning. SEVI introduces an Evidential Safety Critic that estimates both future constraint-violation risk and epistemic uncertainty in a single forward pass. During online learning, SEVI evaluates each task-policy action before execution; if the action is predicted to be unsafe or the prediction is insufficiently supported by evidence, SEVI overrides it with an offline-trained recovery policy that guides the robot toward a lower-risk region. We evaluate SEVI on two simulated manipulation tasks and a real-world USB insertion task under in-distribution and distribution-shifted conditions. Across task-learning and safety-critic baselines, SEVI achieves the strongest trajectory-level safety prediction, the highest final task success, and the lowest unsafe-event counts. SEVI also reduces unsafe events by 13.4 to 57.1 percent relative to recovery-based safe RL while preserving low-latency execution-time filtering. The project page is available at: this https URL


### [227] [TTT in VLA Models: Exploring Training with Human Demonstrations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32094)
*Qiangong Zhou, Jiafeng Liang, Yimin Dong, Yang He, Hongxiang Li*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Long-horizon robot tasks require retaining and using past information. We integrate Test-Time Training (TTT), which stores context in fast weights, into InternVLA-A1 and Pi0.5 to study action generation conditioned on human demonstrations through training on paired demonstrations and robot trajectories. We implement memory transfer between the two phases and truncated backpropagation, and examine training objectives, gradient coverage, and contrastive regularization across video, short-video, and single-frame cue tasks. In our experiments, adding TTT and training on paired data at the current scale did not lead to consistent target selection based on demonstrations. Under the tested settings, shorter sequences, full trajectory gradient connectivity, and contrastive regularization did not clearly improve responsiveness to demonstrations. When demonstrations were reduced to single-frame cues, real-robot execution still showed a preference for cups at fixed positions. We document the implementation, training configurations, and observations to support further inspection and replication.


### [228] [Do VLAs Know They Will Fail? Interpretable Success and Failure Patterns in Physical AI Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32208)
*Christian A. Rogers, Qian Lin, Daniel S. Brown*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models have emerged as powerful general-purpose robot policies, but their successes and failures remain difficult to diagnose and understand. In this work, we show that sparse autoencoder (SAE) representations of OpenVLA activations contain outcome-predictive information and can be used to predict task failure before the robot reaches an unrecoverable state. Moreover, to provide insights into what common behaviors may contribute to these outcomes, we identify individual SAE features whose strong activations are associated with specific stages of success or failure, and uncover recurring behavioral patterns by localizing temporal intervals where these features exhibit significant activation changes. We further develop a VLM-based interpretation pipeline to generate behavioral descriptions of these patterns and validate their correspondence to feature activations on held-out intervals.


### [229] [Conditional Neural Processes for Self-Supervised Goal Discovery in Action Imitation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32688)
*Marco Gabriele Fedozzi, Yukie Nagai, Francesco Rea, Alessandra Sciutti*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Inspired by infants' ability to learn novel tasks by imitating others, in this work we evaluate to what extent Conditional Neural Processes can autonomously learn representations of actions that are informative about their underlying goal. We show that the addition of an attention mechanism in the hidden space fosters the emergence of robust goal representations early on in the observation of an action sequence, making it a promising model for a robotic self-supervised general learner.


### [230] [Adaptive World-Models for Anomaly Recognition (AWARE): A Framework for Robot Dynamics Prediction and Introspection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32742)
*Luke Beddow, George Mavroghenis, Rodrigo Chacon Quesada, Kamil Dreczkowski, Cong Sun, Jiankai Wang, Oscar Kwong-Fai Pang, Antoine Cully*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Unrecognized anomalies in critical infrastructure can rapidly cascade into catastrophic physical or operational failures. Current anomaly detection approaches often rely on direct access to the monitored system by utilizing onboard sensors, such as motor encoders, to detect anomalous states. However, legacy hardware often lacks these sensors, or strict security protocols may prohibit access altogether. We therefore present AWARE, a world model framework that is capable of anomaly detection using only external camera monitoring via system dynamics estimation and state prediction. Evaluated across 240 real-world robotic crane trajectories, including payload collisions and motor degradations, AWARE demonstrates a 26% improvement in anomaly classification over our strongest baseline under noisy, camera-only state estimation. Furthermore, the inferred dynamics parameter can be decoded to identify the source of faults, localizing the degrading motor with 88% accuracy.


### [231] [Semantic Representation for Agentic Self-Play in Automated Laboratories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32755)
*Yves Delley, John Coller, Zachary Cutts, Alexander Daryin, Mathias Bellaiche, Jun W. Kim, Oscar Donosa, Srinidhi B. Seshadri, Tanner Clary, Sandy Klemm, Avinatan Hassidim, Yossi Matias, Pooja Rao, Petar Sirkovic, Annalisa Pawlosky*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Navigating complex, high-dimensional biological search spaces necessitates scalable physical and computational intelligence to explore beyond local optima through iterative self-play across ideation, experimentation, and automation. Translating experimental designs into reliable physical execution remains bottlenecked by static laboratory automation and unmodeled physical variability. To address this, we introduce VERified Infrastructure for Discovery Laboratories, or VERID, a foundational infrastructure designed to bridge the gap between the high-level reasoning of artificial intelligence (AI) agents and complex, error-prone physical realities of wet labs. VERID is a persistent, high-fidelity semantic digital twin of the laboratory workcell that provides four core services: queryable introspection for agentic reasoning, time-compressed simulation for protocol validation, comprehensive trace persistence for a complete scientific record, and a framework for safe execution. We present three case studies using aqueous dye and deionized water mixtures to validate the optical and fluidic foundations of VERID. These studies demonstrate how VERID enables pre-emptive detection of critical errors in experimental protocols, stateful mid-run error recovery and post-hoc identification of subtle physical failures. The simulation functions as a discrete accounting engine, allowing agents to safely and inexpensively explore experimental designs in silico and refine experimental strategies through iterative self-play. VERID also captures structured multimodal experimental data for downstream analysis, knowledge mining and agent training. We argue that this semantic layer is an essential prerequisite for deploying autonomous agents in the biochemical and biomedical sciences, ensuring data integrity, operational safety, and paving the way for tackling previously intractable grand challenges in biology.


### [232] [DexPort: A Hand-Held Skill Interface for Dexterous Hand Data Collection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32969)
*Amber Xie, Jiawei Fu, Xiaofeng Guo, Carmelo Sferrazza, Guanya Shi, Haozhi Qi*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Collecting high-quality demonstrations remains a central bottleneck for learning dexterous manipulation. Teleoperation records executable robot actions, but it requires a complete robot setup and places the burden of coordinating many finger joints on the operator. Human demonstrations are easier to collect, but their motions must be estimated and retargeted to the robot, and they do not directly provide robot actions. In this paper, we introduce DexPort, a portable data-collection interface for high-DoF robot hands. It uses the same hand hardware for collection and deployment, avoiding a hand embodiment gap, and records both commanded joint targets and measured joint states. Our key observation is that a small set of skills can support a wide range of dexterous tasks. During collection, the operator moves the hand-held interface to specify the robot wrist motion and selects or adjusts skills that coordinate the finger motion. This removes the need to control each joint independently while retaining behaviors such as precise grasping and in-hand manipulation. We collect demonstrations across several real-world tasks and in unstructured environments without a robot arm. We then use behavior cloning to train visuomotor policies directly on the recorded wrist motions and hand commands, without skill labels. We evaluate across 4 tasks: pick-and-place with diverse objects, screwing with in-hand tool use, drawer stowage which requires simultaneous grasp and pulling, and a bimanual scanning task. We collect over 2,500 demonstrations with our portable device, in both laboratory and in-the-wild settings. Across our tasks, we see an improvement from 11% to 64% when using recorded robot commands instead of states, and an improvement from 36% to 78% for task success when using in-the-wild data.


### [233] [Input Bias Correction in Gaussian Process Regression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31920)
*Muzaffar Qureshi, Tochukwu E. Ogri, Trivikram Satharasi, Kyle Volle, Rushikesh Kamalapurkar*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: In traditional Gaussian process (GP) regression, the inputs are typically assumed to be known, and uncertainty enters only through the measurements. When scalar fields are mapped using GP regression with measurements obtained from a mobile sensor, the inputs correspond to the estimated pose of the mobile sensor. The goal in this letter is to understand how a recorded GP, trained on erroneous inputs (e.g., pose estimates from dead-reckoning), can be corrected in a numerically efficient way when better estimates of the inputs become available. In the developed bias-aware GP (Ba-GP) framework, the recorded GP posterior-mean gradient is used to propagate estimated input bias and noise into mean and covariance corrections. The resulting additive covariance structure is then exploited to develop numerically efficient first-order correction algorithms. A simulation study and an indoor light-intensity mapping experiment show that Ba-GP improves predictive accuracy and uncertainty calibration relative to noisy-input GP baselines, with an order-of-magnitude reduction in computational cost relative to full GP retraining.


### [234] [RoboCode: Learning Reusable Action Primitives for Robot Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32225)
*Xianyue Peng, Devi Amarsaikhan, Iman Soltani*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Recent robot manipulation policies commonly predict continuous action chunks directly from observations, without explicitly modeling reusable structure in the action space. However, manipulation demonstrations often contain recurring short-horizon motion patterns that are shared across tasks. We introduce \textsc{RoboCode}, which learns these patterns through a shared soft-gated primitive action interface. Rather than assigning each action chunk to a single discrete code, \textsc{RoboCode} represents it as a soft composition of multiple learned primitives, providing a flexible intermediate representation of short-horizon actions. The primitive interface is learned from action chunks pooled across multiple tasks and then frozen while task-specific state encoders are trained to map current state inputs into the shared latent space. Experiments on five ManiSkill manipulation tasks address three questions. First, the learned primitive interface reconstructs action chunks with an average reconstruction MSE of 0.0187. Second, analysis of code activations reveals structured code reuse across tasks. Third, \textsc{RoboCode} achieves the highest or tied closed-loop success rate on all evaluated tasks, with an average success rate of 75.6%, compared with 68.8% for ACT and 41.6% for the larger Diffusion Policy baseline. These results suggest that soft-gated primitive codebooks provide an effective and interpretable intermediate action representation for robot manipulation.


### [235] [When Human Corrections Travel: Safety in Federated VLA Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32888)
*Vigneshwaran Subbaraju, Obaidullah Zaland, Monowar Bhuyan*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Vision-language-action (VLA) policies can adapt to local human intervention, while federated learning can combine distributed robot experience without pooling raw records. We examine their prospective intersection: sharing adaptations derived from diverse human corrections across tasks, environments, people, and robot embodiments. Corrections differ in modality, safety semantics(urgency, behavioral scope, etc.), and privacy sensitivity. A shared update that improves mean task success may suppress a rare safety intervention or produce an unsafe response on another robot. We argue for three requirements: preserve the context and uncertainty of multimodal corrections when deciding what to share; assess candidate updates in each receiving robot's closed loop before deployment; and evaluate leakage alongside retention of safety-critical evidence. Immediate, legible defensive behavior remains necessary during human interaction. We offer counterexamples and a falsifiable evaluation agenda, not experimental results.


### [236] [If at First You Don't Succeed, Backtrack: Lightweight Recovery for Frozen VLAs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32941)
*Thomas Landeg, Thierry Tambe*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models perform well on robotic manipulation, yet remain brittle once their own actions carry them into states their training data barely covers. Recovering from such failures usually requires recovery demonstrations, policy retraining, or a separate model at inference time. We introduce Backtrack, a lightweight failure detection and recovery mechanism for frozen VLAs that needs none of these. A small failure prediction head, trained only on outcome-labeled rollouts of the frozen policy, reads the VLA's existing internal representations to estimate whether the current episode will fail. When the predicted failure probability exceeds a threshold, a controller pauses the policy, retraces the arm's recently achieved motion, and hands control back to the unchanged policy so it can retry from an earlier configuration. The head adds only 0.48-0.88% to the policy's parameter count. We evaluate Backtrack on LIBERO with three VLAs spanning two action-generation families: SmolVLA and $\pi$0.5, which use flow-matching action experts, and the autoregressive $\pi$0-FAST. On LIBERO-Long, the suite of long-horizon multi-step tasks and the one on which all three policies fail most often, Backtrack reduces each policy's failure rate by 19-48%, raising success by up to 7.4 percentage points; on the three shorter suites, where control success is already 85-99%, its effect is smaller. On MetaWorld, all twelve controller configurations tested with SmolVLA improve on the unassisted policy.


<div id='astro-ph.SR'></div>

# astro-ph.SR [[Back]](#toc)

### [237] [ARENA: Active Region attENtion Aggregation for interpretable solar flare prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32758)
*Luis Bauer, Annalena Kofler, Andrés Asensio Ramos, Aparna Venkataramanasastry, Valentín Martínez Pillet, Bernhard Schölkopf*

Main category: astro-ph.SR

Abstract: Predicting solar flares is a challenging space-weather forecasting problem with potentially significant impacts on Earth's upper atmosphere, including disruptions to radio communications. We propose Active Region attENtion Aggregation (ARENA), a neural network architecture that predicts solar flares from a variable number of active-region cutouts while learning to identify the region most relevant to the prediction. ARENA is trained using multiple-instance learning under weak supervision, requiring only a flare/no-flare label for the observation and no labels specifying the flare origin. The learned attention mechanism therefore provides a means of localizing the active region driving the prediction without explicit origin annotations. ARENA achieves a heidke skill score of 0.45 which is comparable to other flare prediction approaches. These results demonstrate that active-region-based aggregation can provide an efficient and interpretable approach to solar flare prediction. The code is available at this https URL .


<div id='cs.CY'></div>

# cs.CY [[Back]](#toc)

### [238] [Generalized green and red concepts for artificial intelligence, big data, and information and communication technologies: Multidimensional framework and countermeasures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31739)
*Jinsong Wu, Yixin Xiao, Haowei Bi, Huamin Fang*

Main category: cs.CY

Abstract: The rapid expansions of artificial intelligence (AI), big data (BD), and information and communication technologies (ICT) are driving substantial growth in electricity, water, and material demands, with data centers projected to consume 6.7--12\% of U.S. electricity by 2028 (up from 4.4\% in 2023) and AI-related water demand estimated at 4--6 billion m$^3$ annually by 2027. Existing ``green AI'' and ``red AI'' discussions have largely emphasized computational efficiency and carbon emissions, providing limited guidance for lifecycle trade-offs, service-versus-use impacts, and broader sustainability externalities beyond energy. To bridge this gap, this paper proposes generalized green AI (GGAI) and generalized red AI (GRAI) and extends the taxonomy to red BD (RBD) and red ICT (RICT) as well as red electronic and electrical engineering (REEE) \& red digital technologies (RDT) under a unified Environmental-Economic-Social-Technical/Operational (E/Ec/S/T) framework. Based on representative footprint indicators in the large-model era (electricity, carbon, water, and e-waste), we analyze how risks propagate across AI-BD-ICT stacks and across service-use boundaries. We further organize actionable countermeasures into a four-tier taxonomy aligned with the Environmental-Economic-Social-Technical/Operational framework: metric-level reform for accountability, revision of information and communication technologies design principles and demand shaping, efficiency-oriented LLM development practices (data, model, training, and scaling), and energy-infrastructure transition. The proposed framework operationalizes the Environmental-Economic-Social-Technical/Operational-aligned assessment and intervention prioritization across the AI-BD-ICT stacks, enabling sustainability-by-design without sacrificing utility.


### [239] [AI Alignment for Digital Democracy: Fairer Voters Representation against LLM Biases](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32780)
*Yi Qi, Srijoni Majumdar, Evangelos Pournaras*

Main category: cs.CY

Abstract: AI representation is emerging in digital democracy, assisting in voter emulation and decision-making. Large language models (LLMs) behave differently depending on the model, the prompt context, and the voter information they receive, representing some groups more accurately than others and potentially making the emulation unfair to certain groups. We investigate demographic biases in LLM-emulated electorates: how accurately LLMs represent individual votes, whether some voter groups are represented more accurately than others, and whether AI alignment can reduce these disparities without sacrificing overall accuracy. Using three U.S. presidential elections, we compare a closed-source and an open-source LLM across voters' demographic features and test AI alignment strategies toward fairer voter representation. Removing gender from the voter profile reduces the largest group error of the models by 22-42%, and removing education reduces it by 64%, whereas removing self-reported race increases the mean group error by over 40%, all without lowering accuracy. Common alignment strategies, such as repeated queries or fairness instructions, do not reduce these errors and can double them.


<div id='physics.comp-ph'></div>

# physics.comp-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [240] [CoPiT: A conditional Position-induced Transformer for aerodynamic field prediction on unstructured meshes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32299)
*Junfeng Chen, Yongchen Fan, Zhonghua Qiao, Kailiang Wu*

Main category: physics.comp-ph

Abstract: High-fidelity Reynolds-averaged Navier--Stokes (RANS) simulations remain a standard tool for aerodynamic analysis, yet each solve on an unstructured mesh is computationally expensive, making many-query tasks such as design exploration, optimization, and multi-condition evaluation costly. Neural operators provide mesh-flexible surrogates for mapping geometry and operating conditions to flow fields; however, in many existing architectures, global parameters such as the freestream state and design variables are concatenated with pointwise inputs, leaving their influence on intermediate features implicit. This paper proposes CoPiT, a \textbf{Co}nditional \textbf{P}osition-induced \textbf{T}ransformer that builds on the Position-induced Transformer (PiT) as its geometric backbone and injects a global condition vector through multi-stage feature-wise linear modulation (FiLM), thereby making parametric modulation explicit while preserving geometry-based attention weights. The model is evaluated on two-dimensional AirfRANS airfoil fields, including scarce-data and extrapolation regimes, and on three-dimensional BlendedNet++ blended-wing-body surfaces. On the AirfRANS full-data test set, CoPiT reduces surface and volume losses by approximately 54\% and 24\% relative to Transolver, respectively, and achieves the lowest overall normalized test MSE among the compared baselines on BlendedNet++. Matched ablations show that FiLM conditioning generally improves upon input concatenation, while the preferred placement is task-dependent. CoPiT thus provides a practical operator-learning surrogate for parametric aerodynamics on unstructured meshes.


<div id='cs.SD'></div>

# cs.SD [[Back]](#toc)

### [241] [MI-MIDI 2: From Observation to Control with Sparse Autoencoders in Text-to-MIDI Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32007)
*Jakub Poćwiardowski, Mateusz Modrzejewski*

Main category: cs.SD

Abstract: Sparse autoencoders (SAEs) can expose features of a generative model without predefined labels, but in language models the evidence used to interpret these features has proven an unreliable guide to which of them steer the output. Whether interpretations of SAE features predict control in music generators has not, to our knowledge, been tested. We develop an end-to-end protocol for discovering and evaluating SAE features in text-to-MIDI generation and apply it to text2midi and MIDI-LLM. The protocol characterizes features through correlations with musical metrics, shared instrument programs, and automated descriptions, each with its own control: note-density adjustment, matched inactive excerpts, and permutation calibration. It then tests candidates by intervention, measured directly in the generated MIDI against unmodified generation and a matched random direction, ranks them in screens, and confirms selected features on new prompts. Evidence that survives these controls points to features that steer the output. Descriptions exceed permutation controls for instrumentation, and a feature whose description names a synth-lead program introduces that program in a prespecified confirmation and in a replication on disjoint prompts and reappears in independently trained dictionaries. Features selected by density-adjusted correlations show a small positive shift of their target metric as a family, whereas features selected by raw correlations show none. An atlas provides MIDI, piano-roll, and audio examples for every feature of the targeted screen.


### [242] [ChoreoStudio: Planning What, When, and Who in Music-Driven Group Choreography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32325)
*Jing Xu, Cunjian Chen, Qiuhong Ke*

Main category: cs.SD
